\vspace{6pt}
{\raggedright\hyphenpenalty=10000\exhyphenpenalty=10000
\fontsize{21}{25.5}\selectfont\bfseries Strong NP-hardness of asymmetric weighted\\ earliness--tardiness scheduling:\\ a machine-checked verification of Part~I\par}

\vspace{10pt}
{\raggedright\large N.\,G.~Hall, H.~Kellerer, M.~Song, \emph{Asymmetric Weighted Earliness--Tardiness: Scheduling with a Nonrestrictive Common Due Date}, arXiv:2609.16046v1, Part~I (Sections~4--9).\par}

\vspace{8pt}
{\raggedright\small\color{muted} Formal development: \lean{AWET_PartI.lean} (one file, 5{,}002 lines, 336 theorems)\\
Lean~4 v4.35.0 \;\textperiodcentered\; Mathlib v4.35.0 \;\textperiodcentered\; October 2026\par}

\vspace{8pt}
\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced,colback=accentsoft,frame hidden,borderline west={3pt}{0pt}{accent},arc=0pt,outer arc=0pt,left=10pt,right=10pt,top=8pt,bottom=8pt]
{\sffamily\bfseries\color{accent} Verdict}\quad
The proof in Part~I is \textbf{correct}. Every step of the reduction from
Restricted Exact Cover by 3-Sets (RXC3) to AWET with the boundary due date $d=\sum_j p_j$
(Sections~6--9) has been formalized in Lean~4 and checked by Lean's kernel. We found no
mathematical error; the minor remarks in Section~\ref{sec:remarks} do not affect the result.

\smallskip
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=14pt,itemsep=3pt,before=\raggedright]
\item \textbf{Formal result} (\lean{AWET.AWET_strong_reduction}). For \emph{every} RXC3 instance $X$:
  $X$ has an exact cover $\iff$ the AWET instance built by the paper's Section~8 has a schedule
  (arbitrary real start times) of cost at most the paper's bound $B=\lfloor A+\Gamma\rfloor$.
  The instance has $8n+1$ jobs, and every number in it is at most $(n+1)^{650}$.
\item \textbf{Check status.} No \lean{sorry}, no added axioms (only Lean's standard
  \lean{propext}, \lean{Classical.choice}, \lean{Quot.sound}), no warnings; the compiled module
  is re-checked declaration by declaration by \lean{leanchecker}.
\item \textbf{Not machine-checked} (taken as given, as in the paper): NP-completeness of RXC3
  (Gonzalez, 1985) and the routine fact that the explicit formulas of the construction can be
  evaluated in polynomial time.
\end{itemize}
\end{tcolorbox}

% ================================================================= 1
\section{What exactly was verified}\label{sec:what}

\subsection{The claim and its logical shape}

Theorem~I.2 states that the decision version of AWET (one machine; positive integer processing
times $p_j$, earliness weights $w^E_j$ and tardiness weights $w^L_j$; common due date
$d=\sum_j p_j$; question: is there a schedule of cost at most $B$?) is strongly NP-complete.
In the standard sense of Garey and Johnson, the paper proves strong NP-hardness with a
transformation $X\mapsto(\mathrm{inst}(X),B(X))$ from RXC3, a problem without numbers, such that
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),leftmargin=22pt]
\item \emph{correctness}: $X$ is a yes-instance $\iff$ $(\mathrm{inst}(X),B(X))$ is a yes-instance;
\item \emph{polynomially bounded numbers}: every number of $(\mathrm{inst}(X),B(X))$ is at most a fixed
  polynomial in the size of $X$;
\item \emph{polynomial time}: the transformation can be computed in polynomial time.
\end{enumerate}
Membership in NP rests on the canonical set formulation (Lemma~I.3, Proposition~I.4): a
yes-instance has an early set $E$ with $\Phi(E)\le B$, and $\Phi(E)$ is computable in
polynomial time.

The Lean development proves (a) for every RXC3 instance, proves (b) with the paper's own
polynomial $(n+1)^{650}$, and proves the canonical formulation used for NP membership.
Item (c) is not formalized: every constructed quantity is an explicit closed formula
(sums over $O(n^2)$ job pairs, roundings, a floor and a ceiling of rational
numbers), and its polynomial-time computability is routine. The NP-completeness of RXC3 is
taken from the literature, exactly as in the paper.

\subsection{The main theorem}

The final theorem of the file is the formal core of Theorem~I.2.

\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inst}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{d}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{n}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℤ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{+}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZca{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{650}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}
\PY{+w}{      }\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Bnd}\PY{n+nb+bp}{|}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{n}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℤ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{+}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZca{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{650}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟨}\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{reduction\PYZus{}iff}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hn}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=\PYZgt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{RXC3}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{size\PYZus{}bound}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hn}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanbox}
Here \lean{X : RXC3} is an arbitrary RXC3 instance with $n$ elements and $n$ sets,
\lean{X.inst} is the AWET instance of Section~8.1 with job type \lean{Job X.n}
($8n$ state jobs and one anchor), \lean{X.Bnd} is the decision bound
$B=\lfloor A+\Gamma\rfloor$ of (8.34), and \lean{X.inst.Yes B} means that some feasible schedule
has cost at most $B$. The first conjunct is correctness (a); the second is the size bound
(b), stated for $n\ge 1$ (for $n=0$ the instance has a single job and $B=0$).

\subsection{The formal problem statement}\label{sec:problem}

A theorem is only as good as its definitions, so the definitions that appear in the main
statement are kept short and close to Definition~I.1.

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Decision version: is there a feasible schedule of cost at most `B`? }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Yes}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{I}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Inst}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{J}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{B}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℤ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∃}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{σ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Sched}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{cost}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{σ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{B}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℝ}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanbox}
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=14pt]
\item A schedule is given by real completion times $C_j$; the start time $C_j-p_j$ must be
  nonnegative, and the processing intervals of any two jobs must not overlap (one machine,
  no preemption). Idle time is allowed anywhere and no job order is fixed in advance, so
  \lean{Yes} quantifies over all feasible schedules of Definition~I.1, not only over
  canonical or integral ones.
\item \lean{cost} is exactly $F=\sum_j w^E_j(d-C_j)^+ + w^L_j(C_j-d)^+$ with $d=\sum_j p_j$.
\item The job set is any finite type \lean{J}; the linear order on it is only used to enumerate
  unordered pairs.
\end{itemize}

The source problem is Definition~I.5:

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ the RPE equations `C\PYZus{}k(x) = k` (Definition I.6) }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{RPE}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Fin}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{n}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Bool}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∀}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{n}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Cpre}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{x}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanbox}
Elements and sets are numbered from $0$; set $v$ is $\{S_{v,0}<S_{v,1}<S_{v,2}\}$, and
\lean{reg} says that every element occurs in exactly three of the $3n$ incidences. The
formalization does not forbid two sets from being equal, so the theorem covers slightly more
inputs than Definition~I.5 requires. \lean{dpre}, \lean{Cpre} and \lean{RPE} are $d_{v,k}$,
$C_k(x)$ and the prefix equations of Definition~I.6 (the first $k$ elements are those with
index $<k$).

\subsection{What the check rests on}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=14pt]
\item \textbf{Kernel and library.} The file is checked by Lean's kernel against Mathlib.
  \texttt{\#print axioms} reports only \lean{propext}, \lean{Classical.choice} and
  \lean{Quot.sound} for the main theorem, for the canonical reformulation and for the
  examples. The file declares no axioms and uses no \lean{sorry}, \lean{admit},
  \lean{native_decide}, \lean{implemented_by} or \lean{unsafe} code.
\item \textbf{Independent replay.} \lean{leanchecker} and \lean{leanchecker-paranoid}
  (shipped with the toolchain) replay every declaration of the compiled module through the
  kernel, independently of the elaborator; both succeed.
\item \textbf{Fidelity of the construction.} The definitions of Section~3 of the file
  transcribe Section~8.1 formula by formula (Table~\ref{tab:construction}). As an external
  check, kernel-evaluated theorems in Section~16 of the file reproduce exact numbers
  printed in the paper's Appendix~B (Tables~10 and~13), and the main theorem decides both
  worked examples correctly (Step~14).
\end{itemize}

% ================================================================= 2
\section{Architecture of the formal proof}\label{sec:architecture}

The formal proof follows the paper's chain of reasoning: canonical reformulation
$\to$ construction $\to$ three gates (anchor early, every block coded, every prefix exact)
$\to$ master identity and threshold. Figure~\ref{fig:map} shows how the main formal results
depend on each other. Where the paper uses a geometric or analytic argument that is awkward
to formalize (schedule surgery in Lemma~I.3, the profile integral in (8.10)), the formal
proof uses an exact discrete replacement. These places are marked ``Difference from the paper''
in Section~\ref{sec:steps}; none of them changes the mathematics of the reduction.

% ================================================================= 3
\section{The proof, step by step}\label{sec:steps}

Each step below states what the paper claims, shows the Lean statement (proof bodies are
elided and the comment gives their length; a few long lines are re-wrapped to fit the page),
and explains how the formal proof goes. Lean names
refer to \lean{AWET_PartI.lean}; Appendix~\ref{app:filemap} lists where each part sits in the file.

% ----------------------------------------------------------------- step 1
\subsection{Step 1 --- Canonical schedules realize \texorpdfstring{$\Phi(E)$}{Phi(E)} (Proposition I.4)}\label{step:canon}

\begin{paperbox}
\paperlabel{Proposition I.4}
For a nonempty early set $E$ with $T=J\setminus E$, the due-date-aligned canonical schedule
(early jobs before $d$ in ratio order, tardy jobs after $d$ in ratio order) is feasible and costs
\[
\Phi(E)=\sum_{\{i,j\}\subseteq E}p_ip_j\min\{r_i,r_j\}+\sum_{\{i,j\}\subseteq T}p_ip_j\min\{s_i,s_j\}+\sum_{i\in T}p_i^2s_i,
\qquad r_i=\tfrac{w^E_i}{p_i},\ s_i=\tfrac{w^L_i}{p_i}.
\]
\end{paperbox}
In Lean the pair coefficients are written without division, since
$p_ip_j\min\{r_i,r_j\}=\min\{p_jw^E_i,\,p_iw^E_j\}$:
\begin{leanbox}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},fontsize=\leanfont]

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ The canonical set objective `Φ(E)` of Proposition I.4 (equation (SO)). }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Φ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{I}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Inst}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{J}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Finset}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{J}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℤ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{k}{else}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{o}{)}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{+}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∑}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{if}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{then}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{else}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{p}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{wL}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanbox}
The canonical schedule is defined by explicit integer completion times. An early job $j$
finishes at $d$ minus the processing of the early jobs placed after it (those with a larger
early ratio, ties broken by index); a tardy job finishes at $d+p_j$ plus the processing of the
tardy jobs placed before it:
\begin{leanbox}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},fontsize=\leanfont]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Completion times of the canonical schedule of `E`: the early jobs end at `d` in}
\PY{l+s+sd}{nondecreasing order of early ratio towards `d`, the tardy jobs start at `d` in nonincreasing}
\PY{l+s+sd}{order of tardy ratio. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{canC}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Finset}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{J}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{J}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℤ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{k}{if}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{then}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{d}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∑}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{filter}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=\PYZgt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{keyE}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZlt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{keyE}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{p}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{k}{else}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{d}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{+}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{p}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{+}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∑}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{n+nb+bp}{ᶜ}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{filter}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=\PYZgt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{keyL}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZlt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{keyL}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{p}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ **Proposition I.4 (feasibility part).** Every early set `E` is realised by a feasible}
\PY{l+s+sd}{schedule whose cost is exactly `Φ(E)`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{exists\PYZus{}sched\PYZus{}cost\PYZus{}eq}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Finset}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{J}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∃}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{σ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Sched}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{cost}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{σ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Φ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℝ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⋯}\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} 1\PYZhy{}line term proof}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanbox}
\textbf{How the proof goes.} Feasibility (\lean{canC_start}, \lean{canC_overlap}) is
bookkeeping with these prefix sums. For the cost, each job contributes $w^E_j$ times the
processing of the early jobs closer to $d$ (or $w^L_j$ times its own and the earlier tardy
processing); regrouping by pairs gives $\Phi(E)$, because when $j$ is closer to $d$ than $i$
the pair contributes $p_jw^E_i=\min\{p_jw^E_i,p_iw^E_j\}$ (\lean{eP_of_keyE_lt}). This is
exactly the exchange-order fact used in the paper. A small generic lemma
(\lean{sum_key_lt_eq}) re-indexes a pair sum along any injective key.

\begin{notebox}
\notelabel{Difference from the paper}
The formal statement also allows $E=\emptyset$ (all jobs tardy, the first one starting at $d$,
which is feasible because $d\ge 0$). This is harmless and slightly simplifies the next step.
\end{notebox}

% ----------------------------------------------------------------- step 2
\subsection{Step 2 --- Every schedule costs at least \texorpdfstring{$\min_E\Phi(E)$}{min Phi(E)} (Lemma I.3, (6.1))}\label{step:lower}

\begin{paperbox}
\paperlabel{Lemma I.3 and (6.1)}
Some optimal schedule has no idle time, a completion at $d$, and both sides in ratio order;
hence $\mathrm{OPT}=\min_{E}\Phi(E)$. The paper proves the structural lemma by closing idle
intervals, shifting a compact block to put a completion at $d$, and adjacent exchanges.
\end{paperbox}
Formalizing these schedule-surgery arguments for real-valued schedules would be laborious.
The formal proof instead establishes the inequality that the paper actually uses: every
feasible schedule costs at least $\Phi(E)$ for some early set $E$.
\begin{leanbox}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},fontsize=\leanfont]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Packing: pairwise disjoint processing intervals inside `[a, b]` have total length at most}
\PY{l+s+sd}{`b }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ a`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}

\PY{+w}{       }\PY{k}{else}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{wL}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℝ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{p}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℝ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{+}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∑}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∈}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{n+nb+bp}{ᶜ}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{filter}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=\PYZgt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{σ}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{C}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZlt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{σ}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{C}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{p}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{i}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℝ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}

\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Φ\PYZus{}le\PYZus{}ordCost}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{σ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Sched}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Finset}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{J}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Φ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℝ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ordCost}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{σ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⋯}\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} 57 lines of tactic proof}

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ **Lemma I.3 }\PY{l+s+sd}{/}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Proposition I.4 (optimality part).** Every feasible schedule costs at least}
\PY{l+s+sd}{`Φ(E)` for some early set `E`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{exists\PYZus{}Φ\PYZus{}le\PYZus{}cost}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{σ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Sched}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∃}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Finset}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{J}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Φ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℝ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{cost}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{σ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⋯}\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} 171 lines of tactic proof}

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ **Canonical reformulation (Lemma I.3 + Proposition I.4).** The AWET decision problem with}
\PY{l+s+sd}{bound `B` has a yes}\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{answer iff some early set `E` has canonical cost `Φ(E) ≤ B`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{yes\PYZus{}iff}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{B}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℤ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Yes}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{B}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{↔}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∃}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Finset}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{J}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{I}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Φ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{B}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⋯}\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} 7 lines of tactic proof}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanbox}
\textbf{How the proof goes.} Fix a schedule $\sigma$ and let $E_0=\{j: C_j\le d\}$.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=20pt,label=(\roman*)]
\item \emph{Packing} (\lean{Sched.pack}): if the processing intervals of a set $F$ of jobs lie
  inside $[a,b]$, then $\sum_{i\in F}p_i\le b-a$ (induction on $F$, removing the job that
  completes last).
\item \lean{ordCost} is the cost of the pair structure induced by the completion order of
  $\sigma$. Every pair term of the form $p_jw^E_i$ is at least
  $\min\{p_jw^E_i,\,p_iw^E_j\}$, hence $\Phi(E)\le\mathrm{ordCost}(\sigma,E)$ for every $E$
  (\lean{Φ_le_ordCost}).
\item If no job is tardy, packing the early jobs that complete after $j$ into $[C_j,d]$ gives
  $w^E_j(d-C_j)\ge w^E_j\sum_{i\in E_0,\,C_i>C_j}p_i$, hence
  $\mathrm{cost}(\sigma)\ge\mathrm{ordCost}(\sigma,E_0)\ge\Phi(E_0)$.
\item Otherwise let $j^\ast$ be the tardy job that completes first and let
  $\theta^+=\max\{0,\,d-(C_{j^\ast}-p_{j^\ast})\}\in[0,p_{j^\ast}]$ be the part of $j^\ast$ that
  straddles $d$. Packing gives, for early $j$ and tardy $j$ respectively,
  \[
  d-C_j\ \ge\ \sum_{\substack{i\in E_0\\ C_i>C_j}}p_i+\theta^+,\qquad
  C_j-d\ \ge\ p_j+\sum_{\substack{i\notin E_0\\ C_i<C_j}}p_i-\theta^+ ,
  \]
  so $\mathrm{cost}(\sigma)\ge\mathrm{ordCost}(\sigma,E_0)+\theta^+(W_E-W_L)$ with
  $W_E=\sum_{E_0}w^E_j$ and $W_L=\sum_{j\notin E_0}w^L_j$.
\item If $W_E\ge W_L$, drop the last term. If $W_E<W_L$, move $j^\ast$ to the early side: an exact
  identity gives
  $\mathrm{ordCost}(\sigma,E_0\cup\{j^\ast\})=\mathrm{ordCost}(\sigma,E_0)+p_{j^\ast}(W_E-W_L)$,
  which is at most $\mathrm{ordCost}(\sigma,E_0)+\theta^+(W_E-W_L)$ because
  $\theta^+\le p_{j^\ast}$ and $W_E-W_L<0$.
\end{enumerate}
Together with Step~1 this gives \lean{yes_iff}: \emph{AWET with bound $B$ is a yes-instance if and
only if $\Phi(E)\le B$ for some early set $E$}. This is the only place where real-valued
schedules appear; everything downstream works with finite sets of jobs and exact integer or
rational arithmetic. It is also the formal content of the NP-membership argument after
Theorem~I.2: the early set $E$ is a certificate.

\begin{notebox}
\notelabel{Difference from the paper}
The formal proof does not use the structural Lemma~I.3 itself (idle-time removal, alignment
with $d$, adjacent exchanges). It replaces it by the lower bound above, which is affine in the
straddle $\theta^+$ and therefore needs no sorting of the schedule. The conclusion used later,
$\mathrm{OPT}=\min_E\Phi(E)$, is the same.
\end{notebox}

% ----------------------------------------------------------------- step 3
\subsection{Step 3 --- Reduction I: exact cover \texorpdfstring{$\iff$}{iff} prefix exactness (Lemmas I.7--I.9)}\label{step:red1}

\begin{paperbox}
\paperlabel{Lemmas I.7--I.9}
With $d_{v,k}=|S_v\cap\{e_1,\dots,e_k\}|$ and $C_k(x)=\sum_v d_{v,k}x_v$:
$C_k(x)-C_{k-1}(x)=\sum_{v:\,e_k\in S_v}x_v$, and $x$ selects an exact cover iff $C_k(x)=k$ for
$k=1,\dots,n$.
\end{paperbox}
\begin{leanbox}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⋯}\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} 6 lines of tactic proof}

\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{sum\PYZus{}dpre}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hk}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{n}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∑}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{dpre}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℤ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⋯}\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} 29 lines of tactic proof}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⋯}\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} 5 lines of tactic proof}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanbox}
\textbf{How the proof goes.} \lean{Cpre_succ} splits the filter defining $d_{v,k+1}$ into
$d_{v,k}$ plus the indicator of ``element $k$ lies in $S_v$''. Both directions of
\lean{exactCover_iff_RPE} then telescope. \lean{sum_dpre} (regularity: the first $k$ elements
carry $3k$ incidences) is used later in (8.13). \lean{n_ge_three} records that a nonempty
instance automatically has $n\ge 3$, which is the standing assumption of Section~8. The formal
reduction goes directly from RXC3 to AWET, so Corollary~I.10 (NP-completeness of RPE) is not
needed.

% ----------------------------------------------------------------- step 4
\subsection{Step 4 --- The construction of Section 8.1}\label{step:construction}

The construction is transcribed definition by definition; Table~\ref{tab:construction} maps the
paper's symbols to Lean names. Indices are $0$-based: block $v\in\{0,\dots,n-1\}$ is the
paper's block $v+1$, and pair $h\in\{0,1,2,3\}$ of a block is the controller ($h=0$) or the
occurrence pair of element $S_{v,h-1}$. A state job is a triple $(v,h,b)$: $b=\mathtt{false}$
is the heavy job $A_{vh}$ and $b=\mathtt{true}$ the light job $B_{vh}$ of Table~2.

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ `N = n + 1` }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}

\begin{notebox}
\notelabel{Formalization details}
(i) $C^\star$ is defined through a \emph{preliminary} instance \lean{preInst} whose anchor
tardiness weight is a placeholder, because $s_a$ (hence $w^L_a$) depends on $C^\star$.
\lean{Φ_pre_eq_inst} proves that both instances give the same $\Phi(E)$ whenever the anchor is
early, which is the paper's remark that ``this cost does not depend on the anchor's tardiness
ratio''. (ii) Where the paper leaves a choice open, the formalization fixes one: the benchmark
coded assignment for $C^\star$ is $\sigma\equiv+1$, ties in (8.17) round up, and $C_0$ is read off
from the benchmark assignment (the theorem \lean{coded_objective} below shows that this is the
constant coefficient of (8.26)).
\end{notebox}

% ----------------------------------------------------------------- step 5
\subsection{Step 5 --- Two zero-load states (Lemma I.11 and (8.4))}\label{step:i11}

\begin{paperbox}
\paperlabel{Lemma I.11, (8.4)}
For a sign vector $c$ on block $v$, $\sum_{i\in B_v}p_ic_i=0$ iff $c=\pm\epsilon$. Equivalently,
the tardy load of block $v$ is $b_v=\beta_v$ iff the block is in one of its two coded
orientations.
\end{paperbox}
\begin{leanbox}

\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanbox}
\textbf{How the proof goes.} \lean{two_states} takes an arbitrary early/tardy pattern
\lean{early} of the eight jobs of block $v$ with tardy load $\beta_v$ and returns the spin
$s$ of the block. Writing the load equation in the digits of $T$, $M$ and the pair codes, the
abstract lemma \lean{digit_decode} peels off the $T$ digit and then the $M$ digit (both use only
$|{\rm lower\ terms}|<$ digit, from $3nM+11113<T$ and $11113<M$). What is left is the code
equation for the four pairs; \lean{pair_decode} solves it by exhaustive case analysis over the
$2^8$ early/tardy patterns (each case closed by \lean{omega}), showing that only the two coded
orientations survive. The converse (\lean{bq_coded}) is a direct computation.

% ----------------------------------------------------------------- step 6
\subsection{Step 6 --- Kernel field and scale separation (Lemmas I.12 and I.14)}\label{step:sep}

\begin{paperbox}
\paperlabel{Lemmas I.12, I.14}
$4T<\beta_v<5T$; $f$ decreases and $0<f_1-f_n<10N^2T$. The physical ratio intervals of complete
clusters are disjoint and ordered like their effective atoms; the bands satisfy
$R_1>\dots>R_n>R_0$, every main gap $\Delta_k$ exceeds $GM$, and $W<15N^3T$.
\end{paperbox}
\begin{leanbox}

\end{leanbox}
\textbf{How the proof goes.} All physical early ratios ($t=1$) and effective ratios ($t=2$)
have the common form \lean{valE t u a Q c} $=f_u+\lambda_u\bigl(t(T+Ma+Q)+c\bigr)$, so the
separation arguments are proved once for both. The relation \lean{above u g v h} says that
cluster $(u,g)$ is at a larger height than $(v,h)$, or at the same height with a smaller block
index. \lean{phys_sep} and \lean{eff_sep} show that both the physical intervals and the
effective atoms respect this order, which is the agreement of orders required by
Lemma~I.13. \lean{eff_gap} and \lean{eff_width} give the gap and width bounds. All numerical
comparisons reduce to the single inequality \lean{scale_key}, which holds because $N\ge4$.
The band-level consequences are \lean{gap_gt} ($\Delta_k>GM$) and \lean{W_le}
($W\le 15N^{53}=15N^3T$).

% ----------------------------------------------------------------- step 7
\subsection{Step 7 --- Exact pair compression and the profile decomposition (Lemma I.13, (8.9)--(8.12))}\label{step:compress}

\begin{paperbox}
\paperlabel{Lemma I.13, (8.10)--(8.12)}
For complete clusters $g,h$ of different blocks whose orders agree,
$I^{\rm phys}(g,h)=m_gm_h\min\{\rho_g,\rho_h\}$. Hence
$J_{uv}=\tfrac14\sum_{g,h}m_{ug}m_{vh}\min\{\rho_{ug},\rho_{vh}\}=\tfrac14\int_0^\infty F_u(t)F_v(t)\,dt$,
and on the $k$-th main gap the profile equals $F_v=d_{v,k}$.
\end{paperbox}

The integral in (8.10) is replaced by an exact finite identity. Every atom position
$x\in[\mathrm{lo}_0,\mathrm{hi}_n]$ is split into its portions inside the bands and inside the
gaps,
\[
x=\mathrm{lo}_0+\sum_{\eta=0}^{n}\mathrm{bp}_\eta(x)+\sum_{\eta=0}^{n-1}\mathrm{gp}_\eta(x),
\]
where $\mathrm{bp}_\eta$ and $\mathrm{gp}_\eta$ clamp $x$ to band $\eta$ and to gap $\eta$. Clamping
commutes with $\min$, so $\min\{\rho,\rho'\}$ splits layer by layer:

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⋯}\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} 33 lines of tactic proof}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanbox}
\textbf{How the proof goes.} On gap $\eta$ an atom contributes the full gap length if it lies
above the gap and $0$ otherwise (\lean{gp_atom}); the signed mass of block $u$ above gap $\eta$
is $d_{u,n-\eta}$ (\lean{Mab_eq_dpre}), which is (8.11). Summing over atom pairs gives the
main-gap term $\sum_\eta \mathrm{gap}_\eta\, d_{u,n-\eta}d_{v,n-\eta}$, that is,
$4J^{\rm main}_{uv}$. For the band term the formal proof uses one more fact: inside one band a
block has at most one atom, because the heights of a block are distinct. Hence the profile of a
block on a band takes only the values $M^{\rm ab}$ and $M^{\rm ab}+M^{\rm at}$, which both lie in
$[0,3]$, and every band contributes between $0$ and $9$ times its width
(\lean{bandSum_bounds}).

\begin{notebox}
\notelabel{Difference from the paper}
The paper bounds the band contribution through $|F_v(t)|\le 3$, which gives
$|J_{uv}-J^{\rm main}_{uv}|\le\frac94W$. The discrete argument gives the sharper
$0\le J_{uv}-J^{\rm main}_{uv}\le\frac94W$ (\lean{Jc_sub_Jmain}). Only the absolute bound is
needed.
\end{notebox}

% ----------------------------------------------------------------- step 8
\subsection{Step 8 --- The objective with an early anchor and the load lock (Lemma I.15, (8.23)--(8.24))}\label{step:lock}

\begin{paperbox}
\paperlabel{Lemma I.15}
With the anchor early, the dominant assignment-dependent part of the objective is
$\frac H2(b-\beta)^\top K(b-\beta)$ (8.23), where $b_v$ is the tardy load of block $v$; by
(8.24) it is at least $H$ for every integer $b\ne\beta$, while all other assignment-dependent
terms oscillate by at most $U-1<H$. Hence every optimum with the anchor early has $b=\beta$.
\end{paperbox}
First, the objective of any early set that contains the anchor is written in terms of state
jobs only. The anchor's early ratio exceeds every state ratio, so each early state job
contributes $p_ap_ir_i=Hw^E_i$:

\textbf{How the proof goes.} The tardy pair coefficient splits exactly as
$\tau_{st}=H\,p_sp_tK_{v(s)v(t)}+p_sp_t\bar\eta_{st}$ (\lean{τ_eq}); this uses the tardy order
$s_i>s_j$ for $\mathrm{blk}(i)<\mathrm{blk}(j)$ (\lean{sS_strict}, \lean{min_sS}). The
$H$-terms of the tardy pairs, the tardy self terms and the anchor interactions combine into
$\frac H2(b-\beta)^\top K(b-\beta)$ plus a constant; as in the paper, the square-processing
terms $q_v$ cancel exactly because $L_v=2\lambda_v$ and $f=K\beta$. Everything else is
collected in $R_0$. The positive definiteness (8.24) and the oscillation bound are:

Here \lean{Lnat} extends $L$ by $L_{n+1}=0$, and \lean{quad_prefix} is (8.24) verbatim. For a
nonzero integer vector $y$, the prefix sum ending at the first nonzero entry equals that entry,
so the corresponding square is at least $1$ and its coefficient at least $2$
(\lean{quad_ge_two}). \lean{R0_bounds} is the statement $\mathrm{osc}(R_0)\le U-1$: each term of
$R_0$ is a coefficient times a $0/1$ indicator, and $U-1$ is exactly the sum of the absolute
values of these coefficients (\lean{U_sub_one}).

\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{anchor\PYZus{}gate}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Finset}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Job}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{n}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hE}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Job}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{anchor}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∉}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Cstar}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZlt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{X}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inst}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Φ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{E}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⋯}\PY{+w}{  }\PY{c+c1}{\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} 33 lines of tactic proof}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanbox}
\textbf{How the proof goes.} \lean{load_lock} compares an anchor-early set $E$ with a wrong
load against the benchmark coded set (spin $\sigma\equiv+1$, load exactly $\beta$, lock
penalty $0$). By \lean{phiQ_decomp}, the difference of the two objectives is at least
$\frac H2\cdot2-(U-1)=H-U+1>0$. \lean{anchor_gate}: if the anchor is tardy, its own tardy term
alone is $p_aw^L_a=H^2s_a=H^2\bigl(1+\lceil C^\star/H^2\rceil\bigr)>C^\star$, and all other
terms of $\Phi$ are nonnegative. Combining \lean{load_lock} with Lemma~I.11 gives
\lean{coded_of_balanced}: a minimizer has the form $E=\mathrm{codedE}(\sigma)$ for a spin vector
$\sigma$.

\begin{notebox}
\notelabel{Difference from the paper}
Lemma~I.15 is stated in the paper for optimal canonical assignments. The formal
\lean{load_lock} is a pointwise strict comparison with one explicit coded set, which is
slightly stronger and is exactly what the gate argument needs.
\end{notebox}

% ----------------------------------------------------------------- step 10
\subsection{Step 10 --- Coded expansion and the master identity (Lemma I.16, (8.26)--(8.31))}\label{step:master}

\begin{paperbox}
\paperlabel{Lemma I.16}
On coded states, $\Phi_\can(\sigma)=C_0+\sum_v(a^\star_v+e_v)\sigma_v+\sum_{u<v}J_{uv}\sigma_u\sigma_v$
(8.26), and with $x_v=(1+\sigma_v)/2$,
\begin{gather*}
\Phi_\can(\sigma)=A+\Pen(x)+R(\sigma),\qquad
\Pen(x)=\sum_{k=1}^n\frac{\Delta_k}{2}\bigl(C_k(x)-k\bigr)^2,\\
R(\sigma)=\sum_ve_v\sigma_v+\sum_{u<v}\bigl(J_{uv}-J^{\rm main}_{uv}\bigr)\sigma_u\sigma_v,\qquad
J^{\rm main}_{uv}=\frac14\sum_{k=1}^n\Delta_kd_{u,k}d_{v,k}.
\end{gather*}
\end{paperbox}
\begin{leanbox}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},fontsize=\leanfont]

\end{leanbox}
\textbf{How the proof goes.} On a coded set the early indicator of state job $s$ is
$x_s=(1+\sigma_{v(s)}\epsilon_s)/2$ (\lean{xE_coded}). Substituting this into $R_0$ and
expanding gives a constant, a linear term and a quadratic term in $\sigma$ (\lean{R0_coded}).
The coefficient of $\sigma_v$ is the baseline field $a^0_v$ of (8.14) (written in ordered-pair
form by \lean{a0_eq}) minus the tardiness-offset contribution $\eta_vD_v/4$, which is
$a^\star_v+e_v$ by the definition (8.18) of $e_v$. The coefficient of $\sigma_u\sigma_v$ is
$J_{uv}$. Because the load-lock term vanishes on coded sets, this is (8.26)
(\lean{coded_objective}); evaluating it at $\sigma\equiv+1$ identifies the constant with
$C_0$. \lean{master} then completes the square prefix by prefix:
$\frac{\Delta_k}8Y_k^2+\frac{\Delta_kk}4Y_k=\frac{\Delta_k}8(Y_k+k)^2-\frac{\Delta_k}8k^2$ with
$Y_k=\sum_vd_{v,k}\sigma_v$, and $Y_k+k=2(C_k(x)-k)$ by (8.13), which uses
$\sum_vd_{v,k}=3k$ (\lean{sum_dpre}).

\textbf{How the proof goes.} \lean{abs_ev_le} uses only $|\round(x)-x|\le\frac12$ and
$D_v>0$. \lean{abs_Res_le} adds the field errors and the band terms of Step~7 over at most
$n^2/2$ block pairs (\lean{count_lt}). For the gap, \lean{four_Gam_lt} proves $4\Gamma<GM$ from
the integer inequality $36n^2W+4\sum_vD_v<8GM$, using $W\le15N^{53}$ and
$D_v=2M(\kappa_1+\kappa_2+\kappa_3)+22134$ (\lean{Dv_eq'}). With \lean{Δ_gt} this gives
$\Delta_{\min}/2>2\Gamma$. Finally \lean{Pen_ge}: one violated prefix equation costs at least
$\Delta_k/2$, because $C_k(x)-k$ is a nonzero integer.

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=14pt]
\item \textbf{If} (Lemma~I.17). Choose the coded set of an exact cover $x$. Its prefix penalty is
  $0$, so the master identity and $|R|\le\Gamma$ give $\Phi\le A+\Gamma$. Since $\Phi$ is an
  integer, $\Phi\le\lfloor A+\Gamma\rfloor=B$ (\lean{Int.le_floor}).
\item \textbf{Only if} (Lemma~I.18). By \lean{yes_iff} some early set has $\Phi\le B$; take a
  minimizer $E_0$ of $\Phi$ over all (finitely many) early sets. Gate~1: the anchor is early,
  otherwise $\Phi(E_0)>C^\star=\Phi(\mathrm{codedE}(+1))$, contradicting minimality. Gate~2:
  every load is $\beta$, otherwise \lean{load_lock} contradicts minimality; so
  $E_0=\mathrm{codedE}(\sigma)$. Gate~3: if a prefix equation fails, then
  $\Phi\ge A+\Delta_k/2-\Gamma>A+\Gamma\ge B$ by the master identity, \lean{Pen_ge},
  \lean{four_Gam_lt} and \lean{Δ_gt}, contradicting $\Phi\le B$. So $\sigma$ solves RPE, and by
  Reduction~I it is an exact cover.
\end{itemize}
The empty instance is handled separately; then the instance has only the anchor, and both
sides are yes-instances:

\begin{notebox}
\notelabel{Difference from the paper}
The paper's only-if proof starts from an optimal schedule and applies Lemma~I.3. The formal
proof starts from a minimizer of $\Phi$ over all early sets, which exists because there are
finitely many, and uses \lean{yes_iff}. Gate~3 needs only $\Delta_{\min}/2>2\Gamma$; the extra
``$+1$'' in (8.33) is not used.
\end{notebox}

% ----------------------------------------------------------------- step 13
\subsection{Step 13 --- Polynomial and unary bounds (Lemma I.19)}\label{step:size}

\begin{paperbox}
\paperlabel{Lemma I.19}
Every integer of the constructed instance is bounded by $N^{650}$, $N=n+1$.
\end{paperbox}
\begin{leanbox}

\textbf{How the proof goes.} The bounds are propagated along the dependency chain of the
construction, exactly as in the paper's Steps~1--5, with a handful of helper lemmas: products
add exponents (\lean{mul_le_pw}), sums over the $8n\le N^3$ state jobs or the $n\le N$ blocks
add $3$ or $1$ to the exponent (\lean{sum_SJ_le}, \lean{sum_blocks_le}), and constants
$c\le4^k$ are absorbed as $N^k$ because $N\ge4$ (\lean{mul_pow_le}). Rational quantities
($a^0$, $a^\star$, $e_v$, $J_{uv}$, $C_0$, $A$, $\Gamma$) are bounded in $\Q$ and transferred to
the floor $B$. Table~\ref{tab:bounds} compares the exponents.

\centering\footnotesize
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4.2pt}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\rowcolors{2}{white}{rowgray}
\begin{tabular}{@{}l l c c@{\hspace{14pt}}l l c c@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Quantity} & \textbf{Lean} & \textbf{Lean} & \textbf{Paper} & \textbf{Quantity} & \textbf{Lean} & \textbf{Lean} & \textbf{Paper}\\
\midrule
$p_i$ (state) & \lean{pS_le} & 51 & 51 & $U$ & \lean{U_le} & 253 & 253 \\

$\lambda_v$ & \lean{lam_le'} & 21 & 21 & $s_i$ & \lean{sS_le} & 277 & 276 \\
$L_v,\ K_{uv}$ & \lean{K_le'} & 22 & 21 & $w^L_i$ (state) & \lean{wLS_le} & 328 & 327 \\
$f_v$ & \lean{f_le} & 75 & 74 & $w^E_a$ & \lean{wEJ_anchor_le} & 331 & 330 \\
$r_i$, $\rho$, $\Delta_k$ & \lean{rS_le} & 76 & 75 & $C^\star$ & \lean{Cstar_le} & 387 & 385 \\

\caption{Exponents $e$ of the bounds $\le N^e$ proved in Lean, next to the paper's exponents
in the proof of Lemma~I.19 (strict bounds $<N^e$). The Lean bookkeeping absorbs constants
more generously, so a few exponents are $1$--$4$ larger; all are far below $650$.}
\label{tab:bounds}

As a point of reference, in the paper's worked example ($n=6$) and in random instances with
$n\le9$, the largest integer of the constructed instance is about $N^{308}$ (the bound $B$),
and the anchor's tardiness weight is about $N^{185}$; the exponent $650$ is very generous.

evaluates it on the two worked examples of Appendix~B with the kernel (\lean{decide}; no
\lean{native_decide}):

\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{exNo\PYZus{}noCover}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{exNo}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasExactCover}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{unfold}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{RXC3}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{HasExactCover}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{RXC3}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{IsExactCover}\PY{n+nb+bp}{;}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{decide}

\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ ... hence its constructed AWET instance has **no** schedule (with arbitrary real start}
\PY{l+s+sd}{times) of cost at most `B`. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{exNo\PYZus{}no}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{¬}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{exNo}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{inst}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Yes}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{exNo}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Bnd}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{fun}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=\PYZgt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{exNo\PYZus{}noCover}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{exNo}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{reduction\PYZus{}iff}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mpr}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanbox}
The five numerical theorems reproduce, digit for digit, the paper's Table~10 (first row) and
Table~13 (the first main gap, the narrowest main gap $\Delta_{\min}=\Delta_4$, and the total band
width $W$). The last two theorems use the main theorem in both directions: the 49-job instance
built from the yes-example has a schedule of cost at most $B$, and the instance built from
the no-example has \emph{no} schedule of cost at most $B$, among all schedules with arbitrary real
start times. This is a statement that no finite computation over schedules could establish.

% ================================================================= 5
\section{Remarks on the paper}\label{sec:remarks}

\textbf{No error was found.} All statements on the path to Theorem~I.2 hold as stated:
Lemma~I.3 and Proposition~I.4 (in the form $\mathrm{OPT}=\min_E\Phi(E)$), Lemmas~I.7--I.9 and
I.11--I.19, and the equations used in between. The construction of Section~8.1 is
well defined and works exactly as described. The remarks below are minor; none of them affects
the result.

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=20pt,itemsep=5pt]
\item \textbf{Small instances.} Section~8 assumes $n\ge3$, ``since smaller instances can be
  decided directly''. For RXC3 this assumption is automatic: a nonempty instance contains a
  set of three distinct elements, so $n\ge3$ (\lean{n_ge_three}). The empty instance
  is a yes-instance, and the construction still produces a valid one-job instance with $B=0$
  that is a yes-instance, so no case distinction is needed in the reduction
  (\lean{reduction_correct_zero}).
\item \textbf{Proposition~I.4 for $E=\emptyset$.} The proposition is stated for nonempty $E$,
  but the all-tardy assignment is also realized by a feasible schedule (start at $d$) of cost
  $\Phi(\emptyset)$. Allowing $E=\emptyset$ is harmless and slightly simplifies the
  argument.
\item \textbf{Choices left open.} The paper allows any coded assignment as the benchmark for
  $C^\star$ (8.25) and either nearest integer in (8.17). The formalization fixes $\sigma\equiv+1$
  and $\round x=\lfloor x+\frac12\rfloor$; the proofs use only the properties the paper
  states. The constant $C_0$ is described as ``the computable constant coefficient'' of (8.26);
  the formalization computes it from the benchmark and proves that this is indeed the constant
  coefficient (\lean{coded_objective}). The paper's bound on $C_0$ by averaging over all sign
  vectors is replaced by a direct bound.
\item \textbf{The ``$+1$'' in (8.33).} Gate~3 only needs $\Delta_{\min}/2>2\Gamma$: then
  $\Phi\ge A+\Delta_{\min}/2-\Gamma>A+\Gamma\ge\lfloor A+\Gamma\rfloor=B$. The additional unit
  ``to accommodate the integer floor'' is not needed (but does no harm).
\item \textbf{Exponent bookkeeping in Lemma~I.19.} The formal bounds differ from the paper's
  intermediate exponents by $1$--$4$ in places (Table~\ref{tab:bounds}) because constants are
  absorbed more coarsely. We did not try to reproduce the paper's sharper intermediate
  exponents, which look plausible; the paper's final claim, that every integer is at most
  $N^{650}$, is confirmed.
\item \textbf{Repeated sets.} Definition~I.5 speaks of a family of sets; the formal reduction is
  correct even if some sets coincide, so it covers a slightly larger class of inputs.
\item \textbf{Proof routes.} Three arguments are replaced by more elementary equivalents in the
  formalization: the schedule surgery in Lemma~I.3 (Step~2), the profile integral in (8.10)
  (Step~7), and the optimality formulation of Lemma~I.15 (Step~9). The paper's arguments for
  these steps are correct as informal proofs; the replacements were chosen because they are
  shorter to formalize.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{What remains outside the formal proof.} (i) The NP-completeness of RXC3
(Gonzalez, 1985), cited by the paper. (ii) The polynomial running time of the
transformation (Lemma~I.19, Step~6): the Lean definitions are explicit formulas over $O(n^2)$
terms whose numbers have $O(\log n)$ bits, and \lean{size_bound} bounds every output number by
$(n+1)^{650}$, so the unary size is polynomial as well; but running time itself is not a formal
statement in the file. (iii) The standard meta-argument (Garey and Johnson) that (a)--(c) of
Section~\ref{sec:what} imply strong NP-hardness. All three are textbook facts that the paper also
takes as given.

% ================================================================= 6
\section{Reproducing the check}\label{sec:repro}

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=14pt,itemsep=4pt]
\item \textbf{Toolchain.} Lean 4 v4.35.0 (commit \lean{eb6cafb7}) and Mathlib at tag
  \lean{v4.35.0} (commit \lean{56a00585}). Any Lake project that depends on this Mathlib
  version will do; the file needs nothing else.
\item \textbf{Command.} \lean{lake env lean AWET_PartI.lean}. On the machine used for this report
  the file compiles in about one minute.
\item \textbf{Expected output.} No errors and no warnings; exactly the three lines
\begin{leanbox}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},fontsize=\leanfont]
\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZsh{}}\PY{n}{print}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{axioms}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{AWET}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{AWET\PYZus{}strong\PYZus{}reduction}
\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZsh{}}\PY{n}{print}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{axioms}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{AWET}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Inst}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{yes\PYZus{}iff}
\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZsh{}}\PY{n}{print}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{axioms}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{AWET}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Examples}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{exNo\PYZus{}no}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanbox}
  each followed by \lean{depends on axioms: [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]}.
\item \textbf{Optional kernel replay.} Compile to an \lean{.olean}
  (\lean{lean -o AWET_PartI.olean AWET_PartI.lean}) and run \lean{leanchecker AWET_PartI}
  with the same \lean{LEAN_PATH}; it exits with code $0$ (about $10$ seconds).
\item \textbf{File fingerprint.} SHA-256 of the verified file: \\
  {\footnotesize\lean{87c9119b2c3b35a716ddab3de0e537cfd5e0f9a6791364f1f15243a6664a0419}}.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Numerical cross-check before formalization.} Independently of Lean, the construction
was first re-implemented in Python with exact rational arithmetic and run on the paper's
yes-example and on random RXC3 instances with $n\in\{3,6,9\}$. For every instance it checked
Lemma~I.11 by enumerating all $2^8$ sign vectors of every block, the claims of Lemmas~I.12 and
I.14, the bounds on $J_{uv}-J^{\rm main}_{uv}$, $e_v$ and $\Gamma$, inequality (8.33), the anchor
gate, and the master identity (8.31) together with $|R(\sigma)|\le\Gamma$ and the correct
yes/no outcome for \emph{every} coded state $\sigma\in\{\pm1\}^n$. All checks passed.
